“I…don't know. That man proved himself capable of a lot. I wouldn’t put children out of his line of fire. I just—I shouldn’t be here. I put them in danger and—oh my god. I put Alex and her baby in danger. I know Maggie would be there but the fear in her could cause—”

“Lena. I don’t want you to follow that spiral. Don’t you see that’s his game? He doesn’t have to take away your happiness when you can find a million reasons to run from it yourself. Not to be tacky but his best weapon against you is yourself.”

“How do I make myself not his weapon?”

“Short answer? Ignoring him. Ultimately not possible, I know. Saying he is persistent and you have people to look out for. I know you have to worry about your employees and loved ones.”

“Not family.” Lena sighed.

“Well—do you think he would ever harm Lillian?”

“It’s nothing he can use against me.”

“Is it not?”

Lena bristled. “She chose her favorite.”

“Lena, you worship at the altar of rejection.” Dr. Quinzel sipped from her mug. “You’ll take the path that ultimately gets you hurt, that will justify to you why you were existing as you were. Think about this: you tried to level with Lex years before, knowing he was too far gone. You wanted to see he was, but hurt yourself further. You’ve done the same with Lillian and not to be too cutthroat, but with Kara as well. You put yourself in a situation you feared would get your rejected. Correct?”

“A single mother? I guess,” Lena said hesitantly. “But I wouldn’t set myself up to fail. That’s too naïve and just a waste of time.”

“It was a younger you thing. I see it has changed, with you willing to fight for Kara.” Harley adjusted her glasses. “Anyways, what I’m trying to say is that I know with that you desperately—secretly—want things to fail and prove you right.”

“I don’t want this one thing to fail. What do I do? Because this man wants it all to fail!” Lena snapped. “What do I do? It’s been years and he’s still out there. What the fuck do I do?”

“Do you think she’ll run? Because you fear him? Why do you think that’s so?” Harley asked, her voice obtaining an empathetic quality.  

“Because…she…I have to be okay when it comes to him. I have to act like the law is doing enough for me to sleep at night. But I—he was here. If not physically, he sent someone. They know about Audra; they know about Kara. I was so afraid of the press knowing that I didn’t think that he would come back,” Lena said, voice tapering off in volume and confidence. She was nearing tears by the end.

“Lena, if you have legitimate concerns for your safety, I, of course, recommend you taking security measures. But in all honesty, I see this coming from paranoia more than concern. I wouldn’t dream of invalidating your worries, I know you’re good at surviving, and you know Lex better than anyone. If you think he’s capable, he might be. But do you think this man would sacrifice his freedom to get his odd sense of satisfaction?”

Lena had a passing thought, a resignation that no one like Harley would see. He’s convinced himself that he’ll get away with it. He always has, in the end.

“No. He much prefers the couple hundred million he embezzled and the Germans he plays chest against in South America,” Lena drawled.

“I suggest hiring some security. I know a private police company here in Gotham run by Brucie’s uncle. They’re international, I’m sure they could help.”

“No, I have my own guards on payroll. I just don’t like having them around besides functions. My taser and self-defense training is most I need. A gun locked up in the house is sufficient.”

“Kara knows about that, right?”

“Yes. It’s safely locked up, so Audra won’t ever get to it. But she…after Jeremiah, I know she’s a bit sensitive on the idea of home invasion,” Lena sighed. “I’ve been so wrapped up in myself…I should worry about that. She has her daughter to worry about, and if we went so far to worry about Audra being taken—” Lena saw how Harley’s face dropped and she decided to stop.

“It’s, of course, possible,” she said, not much emotion in her tone. “Kara has her own issues that I know need to be tended to outside of what you and her family can do. This isn’t the first time he’s been a threat to them.”  

“I don’t think he’ll harm them. I don’t—no, he wouldn’t.” Lena shook her head.

“If you think so,” Harley said.

“I think I feel better. I should get back into all this,” Lena said after a moment.

“Fine. Call me if you feel it getting worse,” Harley sighed. Lena ended the call officially and put aside her laptop, going outside to the porch. Eliza sat out there with Maggie and Alex.

“Where’s Kara?” Lena asked, sitting on the rail that ran in front of the small table they gathered wood chairs around.

“She took a phone call,” Alex said.

“I think it may have been Mon-el. He always checks in,” Eliza said casually.

“You think she would tell him?” Lena asked as she sat down with them.

“She’s always been about being transparent with him,” Maggie said. “Every time she’s dated, if the girl went to the doctor, stuff with school. They don’t have a good relationship, but the dynamic isn’t terrible.”

“What would he do if he knew?”

“That his daughter was indirectly put into risk? That you guys so much as even considered the possibility of Audra coming into harm? He’d have a freakout,” Alex said bluntly.  

Lena grimaced. She felt the favor of her in these people’s eyes fading. She wasn’t surprised that it was caused by Lex. What else would influence it?  

Kara came in, looking frazzled. “I was just talking to Mon-el. He wants this dealt with and processed as it is: a threat of harm. Wants us back and under police protection.”

“Kara I’m technically always under protection. I have armed guards at my building of work and living. Even my driver has been trained to handle a firearm and is well compensated for keeping up with that training. My cars are all armored and my staff heavily vetted.”

Kara sighed and shook her head. “It’s not just you being anxious and our outburst. It’s not about you—it’s Audra and me.” She crossed her arms, shielding herself. She didn’t look Lena in the eye. “Winn has been watching my apartment, watering the plants and getting the mail. He came this morning and the door was open. Only one thing was taken, the shoebox Audra kept all the pen pal letters you wrote. Winn knew it was gone because she always showed it to him when she got a new letter earlier this year.”

Kara frowned before continuing. “There were photos they left. Of us, of Audra. I’m going back home. If Lex is going to involve my daughter in this, I want it figured out.”

Lena stood. “I’ll get the car packed up—”

“No, Mon’s coming with his partner. They’re driving us back.” Kara looked at her. “I want to feel safe when I get back.”

Lena bit her lip. “I…understand. I’ll take a different way back. Will you at least call?”

Kara nodded. She only changed expression to put on a smile when Audra came in. They all put on that mask.

Even Lena, who felt the clock counting down to the end start.

You worship at the altar of rejection.

 Audra came up to Lena. “Is everything okay, Lena?”

“Yes, honey.” She knelt to her level and put her hands on her shoulder.

“Why are we leaving? Mommy said you aren’t coming with us.”

“It’s just because of last night. It was a lot of anxiety and it’s just best we go back to National City a bit early.”

“Are you still scared of the boogeyman?” Audra asked.

Lena looked up, pausing to look at Kara. Kara had her arms crossed, grimacing a bit as she looked at the pair. She caught Lena’s eyes. There was still kindness in her eyes. Even if she was exhausted and scared. Even if Lena was indirectly putting her through her worst nightmare.

Lena took a deep breath and looked at Audra. “I am. We may have to be apart for a while because I’m still scared. But you’ll be safe. You’ll have your daddy and mommy with you.” She forced a smile, brushing Audra’s hair from her face. “And we’ll talk when we can. Just promise me one thing?”

“Yeah?”

Lena cupped her cheek. “Remember that I don’t just love your mommy. I love you. And I’m not going anywhere. Just because I’m away, doesn’t mean I’m gone.”

“Okay.” Audra hugged her. Lena shut her eyes and kissed Audra’s head.

Kara had gotten a call in the morning. She woke up in the bed with Audra, Lena gone. Her suitcase was still on the floor when Kara looked, reassuring her that Lena hadn’t up and left in a panic after what had happened. It was just a phone call. He wants to scare her. It’ll be okay. I just don’t want what Audra said on top of it all to spook her.

Kara turned and looked at Audra, the little girl still sleeping. Her little face was relaxed, half her face smushed into the pillow and her stuffed penguin in her arms. The pink cow got left at home, Winn promising Audra he would make sure the cow didn’t become lonely.

She heard her phone buzzing on the nightstand and she turned away from her daughter to grab it. She sat up and looked at the screen to see Mon-el’s number on the screen. She got out of bed and answered the call downstairs. She smiled a bit at Eliza in the kitchen before she took the call.

“Hello?” She spoke, voice still scratchy from sleep.

“Is there something you’ve been meaning to tell me concerning that Luthor?” He drawled.

She paused. “Mon, where’s this coming from?” She grabbed her cup of tea from the counter, seeing the bag’s string hanging out to indicate it was hers. She walked towards the back of the house as she heard Lena on her laptop in the den.

“He didn’t call you?”

“Who didn’t call me?” She sipped her tea. “Mon-el, stop with the twenty questions and tell me why you’re calling.”

“Your apartment got broken into, Kara! Winn came over to water your plants and he called me.”

She felt her stomach flip. Did this have to do with Lex? If not, what did she lose? What if they had been there when it happened?

She rested on the wall and felt a rising urge to vomit. “What did they take?”

“They didn’t take anything of value. They took the box Audra kept all the photos and letters Lena sent her. That’s not entirely what concerns me. They left you something that’s concerning. I sent it to you.”

She exited from the call screen and checked her messages. There, she saw the photo he sent. On her coffee table were photos. Photos of her and Lena together. Audra was with them in a few. The one on top was through a window. It was Lena holding Audra last night, hard to see due to the lack of light besides the lamp in the hallway that was always on.

She put the call back on. “He called her last night. Made it sound like he was around. He’s not.”

“Someone working for him is. And it seems he has his eyes on you.” He sighed. “I’m calling you not just as a father. I’m calling as a friend and a cop, I want to keep you safe. The safest place is in the city. I already talked, got you police protection. But you have to come home now. Officer Dox and I will be coming up in street clothes to get you tonight. Don’t let Audra know too much. I don’t want her to be scared.”

“I can’t lie to her.”

“Just—figure it out. I’m not the parent watching her right now. Please just cooperate, Kara,” he pleaded.

She shook her head. “What about Lena?”

“I don’t think you should see her until we have things figured out. Find who took these photos at least. It’s not what I have against her, it’s what’s for the best.” He sighed. “I know it’s a lot to ask.”

Kara stepped more towards the front of the house, able to see onto the front deck. Lena sat out there with Eliza, Alex, and Maggie.

“It is. But…my daughter comes first. No one changes that. Call me when you leave. I’ll tell Audra we have to go.”

“Thank you.”

She frowned. “Don’t thank me. I don’t have a choice.” She hung up and let out a shaky breath.

It happened again. What if I can’t protect her from this?

“Mommy?”

Kara looked back and saw Audra standing there. “Are you okay?”

Kara walked over to her and picked her up. “Baby, I’m going to be honest with you.” She pushed Audra’s unbrushed hair from her face. “We have to go home early. I know it’s really fun to be with grandma and Mags and Ally, but we have to go home. Daddy and Winn came to our home, and it was broken into. Nothing got taken, everything’s okay at home. But they’re worried so we have to go back and make sure everything is okay. We’ll be around some of daddy’s friends until it’s all settled. Okay?”

“I don’t wanna leave,” Audra whined. “If it’s okay can we just stay with grandma?”

“No baby, we have to go. I’m sorry. I promise later this summer we can try to come back.”

“I want to stay!” Audra raised her voice. Kara put her down and the girl stomped her feet.

“We can’t. If we could, we would. I don’t want to leave them either,” Kara said, her voice growing stern. That clued it to the girl and she looked down, pouting still.

“What about mom?”

Kara hesitated. “Daddy is coming to pick us up. We’re going back without her.”

“I want to go with mom.”

Kara frowned and she sank to Audra’s level, smoothing the girl’s hair and looking her in the eye. “Audra, she isn’t your mom. Imra is your mom, I’m your mom. Lena isn’t married to me, so she’s not your mom. She doesn’t like hearing that name. She’s just Lena.”

Audra was misty-eyed and pouty, laying on a thick layer of guilt on Kara. Kara grabbed the girl’s shoulders. “It’ll be okay. I promise.”

She stood up and headed for the porch, her heart heavy.

Notes for the Chapter:

The complete assholery of Jon Cryer's Lex Luthor inspired me to get back to this story. Crisis is just so wrought with tragedy and struggle for any sense of hope that I had to write on that level

26. Chapter 26

Lena left first. It was tearful, even if Audra seemed to understand. She still fought, hugging to Lena and asking to go with her. Lena could barely take it, could barely prevent herself from crying as well. She knew she couldn’t be around them much and they really didn’t know when that would end.

Kara helped Lena get Audra pried off of her. Maggie took the wailing girl inside, everyone else following to give Lena and Kara privacy.

Lena crossed her arms as Kara stood there with her hands in her pockets. “It’s not even public and somehow it’s all a mess,” Lena sighed. She looked at Kara, frowning. “I’m sorry—”

“Don’t,” Kara said, cutting her off. “This isn’t your fault. You trying to be happy doesn’t make you the cause of this bastard’s evil.” Kara came close to her. The distance felt charged, the two different poles wanting to come closer. They knew if they got past a certain point, there would be no stopping it.

So, they stayed at distance. Lena’s fingers twitched to hold Kara’s, feel the warmth of her skin and hoped that would be enough to hold her over. Kara wanted to run to her and pick her up in her arms, kiss her and make a scene before it had to appeared to be over.

Lex had to think he won, that he scared Lena back into loneliness.

“I love you,” Lena whispered. “I love Audra. Parts of me are still scared. When people suggest motherhood to me, I clam up. But Audra saying it—I was kinda happy that she trusted me that much. To view me as someone who would protect her as you do. A mom.”

Kara felt her vision blur and her muscles constrict to fight back against tears. “She loves you so much. I love you too.”

Lena sniffled and nodded, forcing a smile. “I’ll see you soon. Before you know it.”

And with that, she left. She watched the house and Kara get smaller and smaller before she turned a corner and it was just an empty road ahead of her.

Kara came downstairs after she finished up her last bit of packing. Mon-el and Brainy had shown up, standing around in their street clothes. Brainy was, well, special. It wasn’t his looks, though he was rather handsome and has an impressive head of hair. What was special about him was his mind. He was on his way to of course be detective, analytical and meticulous. He saw things many people missed.

His personality was, suffice to say, dry if not rather eccentric.

“Ah, Kara. We meet again,” he started with. His hands were in front of him, fingers pressed together.

She nodded. “A pleasure, Brainy.” She looked at Mon-el. He came over and hugged her. She took it. If she had hugged Eliza or Alex, they would say something and she would likely cry. She needed that level of contact, something to comfort her but with not so much emotional trust or involvement that she could break down.

Mon-el was enough to make her feel a minuscule amount of normal.

“She still here?” he asked.

“No. Left this morning. Audra’s pretty wound up by it. When Lena left, I explained it to her as much as I could. I’m off for another week so we can begin to figure this out before that all comes into play,” she told him after they parted.

He nodded. “Let’s get things packed up now.”

They moved about getting Audra and Kara’s things into the hatchback they brought. It was Mon-el’s, already having Audra’s car seat in it. Kara was thankful for it; she never thought to ask Lena to take the other one out of her car.

Audra fought, even more, when they had to leave. Alex and Maggie planned to stay another few days and Audra realized it was just them leaving. That made it worse. She was dripping snot onto Eliza’s shoulder as the woman held her. Kara worked to coaxing Audra away with gentle words and Eliza having to promise to visit National City before the summer ended.

Audra and Kara sat in the back together. Brainy played terrible classical music and Mon-el seemed indifferent after years of knowing the man. Kara, already on edge, eventually spoke in the silent ride.

Audra was thankfully wearing headphones as her mother said, “I’ll walk the rest of the fucking way if you don’t change that.”

Mon-el pressed his lips together as he leaned over and switched from CD to FM radio. They settled on a classic rock station for the rest of the drive.

They got back to Kara’s apartment by the mid-afternoon. Mon-el got out of the car, adjusting his pantleg to cover his gun. He offered to take Audra to get something to eat as Kara settled in. She let it fly. She knew Audra would always be safe with Mon-el, but it took her ignoring her gut feeling to let him take her.

Brainy and her carried her bags upstairs. “Oh, the lock change,” Brainy said when she stopped to study the new doorknob. He put down the bags he held and took out a silver key, handing it to her.

“You changed my locks,” she said.

“Your friend Winn, actually. He suggested one of those with a camera but I felt it was too easy to hack and allow another entrance by someone so advanced as Lex,” he said, his tone dry and unaware of how morbid that was.

“Yeah…” she sighed, turning the key around in her hand. She looked at the door, the corridor between her and her daughter’s safe haven and the real world. It had been violated. Just like twelve years before.

No, thirteen. Kara mused to herself. Or at least by the end of the summer.

She hesitated to enter, opening the door with shaky hands. She entered and almost punched the person who came up to her. Their tall figure loomed over her and she didn’t look up to see she was hitting.

James caught her swing and grunted a bit as some of the force landed into his gut.

“Kara…it’s okay,” Winn said, coming up as she staggered back. She almost ran into his arms, hugging him tightly. “Hey, it’s all gone. I made sure everything was cleaned up after they were done. You’ll be okay.” They let each other go. “Where’s Lena?”

Kara looked aside. “As far as anyone’s concerned…we’re broken up until further notice.”

His face fell. “I see.”

“Can we please just…I want to feel like I’m normal. I don’t know how I can have that—I just want to breathe,” Kara told the three men around them.

“I’ll go,” James said. He touched Winn’s arm before he stepped out. Brainy studied him briefly. James stopped. “I think you’re the stranger here, my man.”

Brainy’s brows raised. “Well, I have to keep Kara safe.”

“Come with me. We can wait downstairs. Trust me, she can take of herself if someone comes up here,” James said, putting his large and strong hand on Brainy’s shoulder. It came down with a dull clap, making Brainy jump in either surprise or pain. Possibly both.

They left and Winn stood before her. “I’ll go then,” he said quietly.

“Winn.” She grabbed his hand. “I…what did it look like? Mon said you found it. Why didn’t you call me first?”

He frowned. “I hate to admit it. I know you love her but I just felt so—” He stopped and sighed. “—I felt helpless. I promised to protect you and Audra. And he just…came in.”

“Yeah, that’s what he does. Takes happiness away.” Kara frowned. “Lena freaked out when he called. She couldn’t sleep, woke up screaming and crying because she had a dream of him taking us away. I told her it would be okay.”

“And it’s not.”

“No. It’s far from okay. I don’t know what I’m supposed to do,” Kara said. “I can deal with him fucking with her. I can tell her he’s just playing games. She can see a therapist and she can convince her that he wants her to feel scared and she shouldn’t ne. But what am I supposed to do when it’s us? This isn’t letters, staying countries away. This is someone broke into here and went into my daughter’s room. They watched us at my mother’s home. I can’t call him a ghost. I can’t act like we’re safe. I don’t fucking know. Should I even be here?”

He pulled her in for a hug again. She shuddered and wrapped her arms around him tightly. “You’ll be safe. I promise.”

“You can’t promise that. What if Mon-el stops being helpful and I lose her?”

“He won’t do that.” Winn sat her down on the couch. “I’m gonna make you some tea, okay?” He went into the kitchen. She could hear him clattering around, the water running into the teapot, the ignitor on the oven. “So, how are you going to act?”

“I have to act normal, I guess. Like I don’t know her, don’t love her. She has to act like she doesn’t love me.” She messed around with the hole in her jeans, picking at the loose threads stretched over her knee.

 “What was your normal before her, you think?”

Kara frowned. “Fear. Anger. In all things but you guys and Audra. Only five people that didn’t make me worry it would all go away. Well, four. Audra, I feared would go away. There were days I worried my life I so carefully built of toothpicks and cotton would be blown to bits by Mon-el’s frustrated huff. Or his mother’s. Still his mother’s.”

Winn touched her back. She leaned into him. “I don’t want that back.” She bit her lip. “I thought I could have it all. There was this one morning when we were leaving. God, Winn it was so perfect.” She sucked in a deep breath as she felt her body choke up. “I woke up first and I got to see them cuddling. They were like two puzzle pieces. She doesn’t even do that to Maggie. Only Alex and you. And Lena—” She looked ahead, not wanting to meet his eye as she rambled on. “She woke up all groggy and Audra did too. I found out Audra had a nightmare and she…Lena was the person that got to her. She told Audra she was safe because she had all of us. Had her too. We functioned as a unit. As a…”

“Family?” Winn offered her a tissue. She hesitated and touched her face, realizing she had begun to cry.

“Yeah.” She took it and dabbed at her eyes. “I didn’t want to push her aside. I love her.”

“I know.” He hugged her close. “You can cry. In a few minutes, you have to act okay.”

She nodded, burying her face in his sweater and gripping a handful of it in her hand.

Lena got into her office the next day. She walked in and everyone looked at her with a level of shock and they changed their demeanor. People were less intimidated by a woman like Sam. Though, Lena didn’t know she inspired a level of fear in people.  Or maybe it was the air around her and the surprise of her being a week early.

When she got to her office, pushing open her doors, Sam was ready for her. She stood in the middle of the room, arms crossed. “I told you things would be okay—” Sam started, assuming this was paranoia due to it being Lena’s first vacation of such a long stretch of time.

“They’re not.” Lena went to the small pile of mail for her that Sam stored. She dug through and pulled out a parcel that had several stamps from various countries. She tore it open and pulled out the stack of photos. She slapped them down on the desk and splayed them out. They were from her and Kara’s time at the park with Mon-el this time. Another was Kara and Lena sat together at a lunch. The air around them was worry-free. Ignorant.

It wouldn’t be like that anymore.

Sam came over and looked at them, pushing them aside to see others below. “Lex,” was all Sam could muster to say.

“He called me. He had eyes on me. Has them on them,” Lena told her. She frowned. “I was so foolish. I thought it would just be me. That I could take some postcards and threatening packages. The odd phone calls. But this is a threat. He wanted me to know he knows. He knows I’m trying to get better and he can’t have that.”

“Where’s Kara?” Sam asked.

“I don’t know. That’s the point. I’m not going to spend my time trying to find out.”

“Well, how are you going to protect her or catch him? If he’s looking at her and you,” Sam said.

Lena shook her head. “She didn’t want me to protect her,” she said, her voice cracking. She stepped up and looked out the window. She wondered if there was a drone somewhere, looking to find a time where she had her eyes downcast and it could come and watch her. “It’s not just me anymore.”

“So, what do you do?” Sam asked.

“I…leave her. She made her point of view apparent.” Lena looked over at Sam. “I can live with it. I did before.”

“I know that’s a lie.” Sam came up to her side, touching her shoulder. “If you love her, fight for her.”

Lena pressed her lips together to keep the lower one from trembling. She looked away. “I love her. Sam, I can’t even tell you. How fast I could…it went against every part of my being. I never let anyone in after everything. But then she came. Honest, caring, and guarded like me. She told me so much because she wanted to make sure I wouldn’t leave once I got wise. I just wanted more. So much more…” She took a delicate breath, not wanting to have it hitch.

“Then get more.”

Lena shook her head. “I won’t take. Not when that man is still in the equation. I can’t do anything to catch him. He’s made that apparent.”

“There’s so much to be done—” Sam started. She was stopped by a hand on her sternum.

“There’s nothing. Not with Lex.” Lena stepped away. “I think it’s time you got back to Ruby.” She went to her bar, pouring herself a glass of bourbon.

Sam left, the door slamming behind her. She sent a text to Lena after she left.

You really need to stop letting him win.

Lena sank behind her desk and sipped her drink. She set it gently on the glass surface. She stared off, glaring at the ugly painting that was a gift from someone on the board. Lex’s microphones were in that.

27. Without You

Summary for the Chapter:

What do you do when your worst anxiety is your reality?

Lena arrived at the state prison in the early morning. She had taken a red-eye flight, her eyes still bleary with the few hours of sleep she got on the plane. She had Harley’s voice in her head, telling her to turn back. Pleading with her that this wasn’t worth sacrificing all the work they had put in.

But Lena adjusted her hold on the box under her arm and she walked into the visitor's entrance. They patted her down, made her walk through a metal detector, x-rayed her chessboard.

She got admitted in. She came in and saw her adoptive mother sitting alone at a metal octagonal table. She looked up. “What have I done to earn the time of the prodigal Lena Luthor?” Lillian quipped, smirking.

Lena sat down and opened the box, the pieces spilling out. “I figured you’re bored. Maybe play a game and catch up.” Lena set up the pieces, making Lillian the white.

“This is about Lex and your little girlfriend?”

Lena fumbled the pieces, knocking over her queen and a few pawns. She took a deep breath and fixed them.

“I never understood your preference for those of a more delicate disposition,” Lillian said. “Is it maybe because of Cecilia?”

Lena set her jaw. Ignore her taunts. She wants a reaction. “I think Lionel maybe. He loved women so much he wanted more than one.”

Lillian smiled a bit and sat back. “So, you’re here to see what I know about your brother. You just want to get him caught, sent to death. Then you can go back to that reporter and her child.” Lillian sat back. “It’s terrible how hard I tried to teach you to be strong, you’re weak.”

Lena looked at her. “Mother, I’m asking just for a game. It’s your birthday in a few weeks, I wanted to give you the chance to see your only child who can see you.”

“Fine.” Lillian started as she always did, moving her left knight.

Lena moved a pawn. Her mind went to when she tried teaching Audra shortly before they left for Eliza’s.

The girl got frustrated but tried really hard. It was the one game Lena didn’t let her win, explaining to her at every step why she had lost the piece and how to think ahead. It was a lot for a little kid, but Lena had learned a few years younger. She knew Audra was bright enough. She is. By the time Lena and she were finished, she was able to beat Winn and James. Lena knew Kara threw the game to make her girl happy. But Kara was someone that Lena would play and they somehow would reach a draw most of the time.

Lena scowled a bit when her mind went to Kara. She had to put those things aside. Finding Lex was her being tired of him thinking he could be bold again. She tried not to think of working towards the goal of getting Kara back. Things would get murky. It would make the process longer.

“I know you’re a Luthor because you have ulterior motives to this. This isn’t out of the goodness of your heart,” Lillian said after a few moves. Lillian had taken the first piece, a pawn taking one of Lena’s.

“I know he’s talked to you already. If you know he threatened the life of an innocent woman and child.”

“I don’t see them as innocent.”  

Lena looked up. Lillian arched a brow. “What would make them guilty?”

“By association.”

“To me,” Lena finished.

The game went on, more pieces being surrendered to the other. “How does he contact you? They wouldn’t let mail in if it was from your outlaw son.”

“Lex always finds a way. That boy would use a carrier pigeon if he had to.”

Lena nodded. She figured there must be someone on the inside helping Lillian. She could spend months trying to find that person, they’d be gone by then. They probably didn’t even know where Lex is.

I know Lex won’t come out unless he has to. What would make him? Lena moved a piece.

“Are you tired, Lena? You look exhausted,” Lillian asked.

Lena blinked and looked up at her. She hesitated and wondered if she misheard Lillian. Was there actually concern in her voice?

“No. I…maybe there’s been some all-nighters. That’s expected. Dad slept in his office half of the time.” Lena rubbed her temples, suddenly aware of how on edge she was.

“It’s a huge workload. Expansion is just more work and you don’t trust people enough to let them help you.”

“You weren’t a psychologist. What would you know?”

“Call it a mother’s intuition,” Lillian hummed. She moved a piece. Lena took the piece she moved the next move after a moment of deliberation.

“Maybe. I consider maybe selling instead of buying next time. But people don’t care as much.”

“Your father wasn’t the caring man you thought he was,” Lillian told her. Lena glared at her, staying silent. “You don’t know the story? He gave Lex control of a plant that was a failure. He wanted to see your brother fail, struggle. Take him down a notch. Instead…Lex turned a profit in the first year. He sold the plant out of spite. Even if it hurts people.”

Lena shook her head. “I know that story. Mother, Lex has told me all the sob stories possible. Being screwed over doesn’t give you a free pass to be a murderer and a terrorist.”

“I’m not trying to say I think your brother’s actions were of valor, merely knowing why Lex worked so hard. It was to prove something.”

“Well, he undid all that work the second he chose to start dealing in arms, to start being unable to take criticism. My empathy ends at his infamy.”

Lillian arched a brow, smirking. “You empathized?”

“I didn’t hate him when he starting talking about conspiracies. Didn’t hate him when he took money from my projects and funneled them into weapons and explosives. I didn’t hate him when he said I got what I deserved when my fiancé outed me. Hell, I didn’t hate him when he sent those bombs for a moment. He just wanted to scare people. You wanted to as well, as you get off on control.”

Lillian held up a hand. “I get no sexual gain from what I did. That’s such a primitive, male thing,” Lillian said.

“You got a high from it. You liked instilling fear and misery. You convinced me if I loved a woman, I would lose every penny, lose all I knew.”

“Didn’t you?” Lillian looked at her. “What do you still have from before you made that selfish choice?”

Lena pounded a fist down on the table. “I had nothing to lose. You just convinced me I did. I have lost something now. And it’s because of him, again. I lost pride before, that stung. I fight tirelessly to get it back. But…this time it was worse. It wasn’t abstract. I had something real. Something so tangible and strong and amazing. Something I know you two will never have. And you do too. And that destroyed you both.”

Lillian didn’t flinch. She began to crack a smile. Lena sat back down and looked down. She had failed, and that feeling washed over her like hot lead. She wordlessly gathered the board and went to leave.

Lillian grabbed her wrist to stop her. She leaned up as she pulled Lena down. “You know, Lena, I always thought I would see a day you stopped fearing me. I prodded, jabbed, cut, lied, cheated, I kept you from your own father—yet you didn’t push back. Maybe putting me in here is seen like that. But no. You did that because you think you have a moral obligation to the system. You play it well. But you’re just a little girl in a costume, desperately trying to show herself she isn’t afraid anymore. But you are. Even more so. Because you’re willing to obtain something to lose.”

Lena wrenched herself from her mother and moved towards the exit. The guards came in and grabbed Lillian, cuffing her before going to her solitary cell.

Lena got into her car minutes later and gripped the wheel. She looked around, touching the steering wheel and dash, running over the stitching in the leather. She grabbed her phone when she felt grounded enough, opening it and looking at her texts.

Her hand hovered over the recent conversation with Kara. Her brain fought between the logic of it would break the façade of them being over and the longing clawing in her chest like a caged animal, aching into her sides and the base of her neck. She put the phone into her purse again and drove home, a ghost behind the wheel.

Lena sat at her desk as the security guards talked amongst themselves. The captain sent off her officers and approached the desk. “You’re all clear now, Miss Luthor.”

“Thank you.” Lena took the document the woman help out, signing it. “I had a hunch some were missed previously.”

The other woman paused. “Miss Luthor, I think you should consider taking protection. Of course, my staff is capable, but I don’t want to be the ones liable if you come under any harm at Lex’s will.”

“I suppose your contract doesn’t have sororicide in it, does it?”

“Not last time I checked.”

Lena frowned. “Very well.” She leaned back in her chair. She looked at the fizzing water next to her. She had cleared all the alcohol from her office and house. This was the second sweep she had done. Lena sighed. “Captain Ferguson? Thank you for your help. I have some other work to attend to, so…” Lena motioned towards the door.

“Right. You stay safe, Miss Luthor. I’m always one button press away.” She walked out, leaving Lena alone for the moment.

Lena checked her phone. She had given the number to the respective agencies. Tracking it was impossible. When the call ended, the phone was destroyed.

Lena opened her laptop and waited for the facetime call to come in. She accepted and Dr. Quinn popped up on her screen.

The doctor stared for a moment before saying, “what happened to you?”

“Lex happened. You were right, Harley, I get rejected. I always—” She cut herself off with a gasp, holding back tears.

Harley’s stoic face shifted to worried, her brows pressing towards her nose, the corners of her mouth turning down, and she leaned towards the computer as if she wished to touch Lena’s hand at the moment.

“I can’t be with her because I love her. I’d never forgive myself if I hurt her. I wish I could just—anything. I’d do anything for this hell to be over.”

Harley looked at her, “I hope that anything is perfectly legal, Lena.”

“I’m in no place to work outside the law. What good would it do me now?” Lena cradled her cheek in her hand, propping her elbow on the arm of her desk chair.

“Well, I just want to know if I have to prepare to stop you.” Harley smiled a bit. Lena didn’t respond, staring almost past the woman.

Harley sighed and she sat up straight. Lena could see behind her that she was at her home office. There was her and her wife’s degrees and doctorates, the next one more grandiose than the other, and the photos to match.

“It was too early for me to think of forever, wasn’t it?” Lena asked suddenly, stopping Dr. Quinn before she could jump into a calm tirade with intentions to part another layer of Lena’s consciousness. Instead, the psychiatrist could only sit back again and look at Lena to see her level of seriousness.

It was not seriousness, but rather ironic sadness. Harley saw it, the sadness that came with Lena starting to grow after two decades of being beaten down, just for her brother to undo all the work and send her tumbling back down. How the hell do you treat that? There wasn’t a drug and there are no breathing exercises. This wasn’t about taking a step back or speaking her mind.

How do you help a girl who just had all the anxieties you talked her down from coming true? How do you, as a physician, deal with a fantastic worst case that you never thought of?

“What do you mean?”

Lena looked down at her desk and sighed.  “I don’t like talking about this. It’s too new. Everything else, it either wasn’t real or years ago. This is just—” Lena’s voice broke, “it doesn’t feel real. One minute I’m on the beach watching Audra and her play in the sand. I’m reading a book next to her mother, I had a family. I had never done anything like that as a kid, you know? My mother wasn’t playing with me, wasn’t getting dirty. Kara’s the most amazing mother, you know?” Lena smiled softly. “She’s so laser-focused and willing. She’d give up anything for Audra.”

“You’re angry she had to give you up, aren’t you?”

Lena snapped out of her trance and her face shifted. Her eyes narrowed and she almost snarled, her lips curling up in disgust. She spoke lowly, “I’m angry that I brought this on her. That the only way I can protect the woman I love is to just…stay the hell away from her. It’s the worst. At least when someone walked away or I had to do it, I knew why. I could take it. My fiancé left because I was gay and I couldn’t love him. My dad left because he died. My brother is a terrorist, a traitor, and a murderer, and my mother was an abusive narcissist who only cared about Lex more because he was her blood. They were messed up. I was messed up.” Lena looked past the screen, rubbing her eyes as she felt tears well up. She tipped her head up slightly, sniffling as her angered expression fell into a hopeless one.

Lena continued, “But she still loves me. I still love her. We’re alive. We’re not evil. I love her daughter. Her daughter loves me." 

Harley just stared as she watched Lena break down more and more, still talking. She rambled more about Kara, "I—I met her more than once. I saw her everywhere. I wanted to see her walk away so many times, Harley. So often I saw the outs. The times that I—if I just said no, that-that the balloon would be just a balloon, and it would have floated to someone else. I could have not spoken to the stressed-out mom that was just cursed out by a man, I could have just let her buy her coffee and go. I could have not stared at my friend’s assistant, could have not given her a dress she looked so much better than me in. I could have ignored her letters and just written to Audra, let that project end. Not sent those tickets and insist on meeting. Said no to dinners, said no to coming over, meeting her friends, and sleeping over.”

Lena covered her mouth, her voice cracking as she went on, “I never said no to her, Harley. She never said no. But when we were saying goodbye and I stopped being logical, asked to have her stay, let me protect her. The one time I didn’t want to take the out in front of me—and she does.”

“Do you resent her for taking it, Lena?” Harley asked.

“No! No…” Lena dried her eyes and her shoulders slumped. She sniffled and took a deep breath, “I just hate that this time I couldn’t accept someone walking away. And it’s not like she even did. This isn’t her fault.”

“It’s not yours either,” Dr. Quinzel fired back at her. Lena looked at her silently. “Lena, say that for me. “This wasn’t my fault,” okay? Say it to me.”

Lena whispered, “not my fault.”

“What was that?”

She lifted her head slightly. “It’s not my fault.”

“What isn’t your fault?” Harley raised her

Lena sighed. “It’s not my fault that Lex is targeting me and Kara. But if I never—”

“Ah! Shut it,” Harley cut, holding her hand up. “Say it now, without the but!”

Lena looked at the small icon that was herself.  She spoke again, “This isn’t my fault. It’s Lex’s.”

“Yes. Good.” Harley looked at her. “Do whatever you need to do, make time for you to rest. Stay clean, and you call me if something changes. You can take that short release all you need, okay? This is a marathon, Lena. Not a sprint. Right now we’re going up a hill we didn’t see on the horizon. You understand me?”

“I don’t need your metaphors, Dr. Quinzel. But yes, I do.” Lena sighed. “I have to go. Same time next week.”

“Good. Remember, I have friends all over. You skip out, you get one of my henchmen at your door.”  

The two signed off and Lena shut her laptop, leaning back in her chair.  

"This isn't my fault," Lena whispered. She looked at her blank laptop screen and reached towards the mouse pad. She repeated it, "this isn't my fault."

She pulled herself closer to her desk and take a deep breath. "I can fix this or let it ruin me," she said to herself. She looked over at her partially opened desk, looking in to see a photo strip. She picked it up and looked down, seeing the four photos of her, Kara, and Audra. 

There isn't an out. I don't want one. 

Notes for the Chapter:

So I'm not dead. I know it's been a year and a half, and something really big happened. I wrote myself into a corner with this story. I know my ending, just not the middle. But I want to finish this story before the series ends. I just can't let one of my favorite works going unfinished.

(also I'm a little rusty so don't fault the typos)

Thanks for reading

28. I'm Not a Victim

Summary for the Chapter:

How Kara lives on

Notes for the Chapter:

I call this chapter me taking every chance to have everyone know Kara has muscles.

Two weeks passed. Kara went back to work as did Lena. Audra went to daycare, taken there by Kara each day. A police car was a lane over and a few cars back. It turned left when she went straight. An unmarked one stayed the same way as her. It seemed like overkill and Kara pointed this out several times. She would get the same reply: “not when a Federal crime is involved.”

She gave Audra a kiss on the head before she sent her off. She drove to work, still being tailed. She got out and went upstairs, taking a deep breath before the doors opened. Winn came to greet her. He had a panic button and so did Kara. Cat hadn’t been informed, but her building’s security team was.

“Hey, how are you doing?” Winn asked her. No one in the group was allowed to talk to Lena. Alex wasn’t even allowed to through her job. Alex didn’t tell Kara anything that she knew about anything having to do with the Luthors.

“Functional,” she told him.

She slept like shit for the past two weeks. She tried everything to fall asleep short of the vices. Her nightmares came back. Instead of reliving the robbery, feeling that helplessness again, she sees something new but similar. She’s Jeremiah, wielding a weapon and a sense of protectiveness. Audra begs her to not go and to hide with her. Kara never stays with her. She goes outside, she’s able to kill the first two intruders. The third one comes up and holds his gun up.

Kara always woke up to the sound of gunfire ringing in her head. She’d gasp and she'd check her body for wounds. Audra was usually sleeping with her. She tried to keep quiet, would sometimes leave the room and sleep on the couch so she could silently break down in peace.

She lost weight. No one had the audacity to say anything. Maggie offered to take time off, watch Audra so Kara had some peace of mind. What better than daycare? Time with your cop aunt.

Kara refused it. It was too much to ask. Maggie was saving her paid days and personal time. She needed it when the baby came. Maternity leave wasn’t progressive enough to include the not pregnant mother in a same-sex relationship.

Kara tried not to think of Lena. She hated that she got used to having the woman in her bed. She hated her anxiety for causing invasive thoughts.

They made her think of Lena. Was she drinking? Was she in danger? Is she still seeing Harley and getting better? Is she seeing someone else?

Kara shut her eyes and her office door. Winn went to his desk after that and Kara took out her laptop, beginning to digitize the notes she took in her notebook from her recent interviews.

After about forty minutes and a cup of coffee, Kara heard a knock at her door.

“Come in,” she called, shutting her laptop partially as the person came in.

Nia came in slowly and shut the door behind her.  “Hey, I just wanted to see how you’ve been doing.” She smiled kindly, though slightly uncomfortable. Kara didn’t blame her, she was young.

Kara rested her elbow on her desk and her head on her hand. “I’m hanging in there. You know—got back from vacation a few weeks ago.”

“Yeah, Winn said you were going with Audra to your mom’s, right?”

“Adoptive mom, yeah. Eliza. I was...” She trailed off a bit and looked down. She cleared her throat. “I was taking Lena to meet her. But there was something complicated.”

“Oh no, meet the parents disaster?” Nia joked likely. Her smile faded when she saw Kara’s face was unchanging. “Oh, something really bad happened.”

Kara sighed. “Nia, you know more than most people in my life, but there is a complication I can’t talk about. It’s for your own good, but let’s just be concise—Lena and I are no longer in a relationship. I was not ready for it to be over. I’m handling it.”

“Okay. So, what I’m hearing is that lunch is on me, and I’m bringing you wine tonight. And women suck.”

Kara sat back in her chair and grinned a bit. “Pardon my asking, but are you straight Nia?”

“No, I’m not deranged,” Nia smirked back at her. 

Kara snorted and covered her mouth. “Okay. Just one warning—come after eight-thirty. That’s Audra’s bedtime.”

“Okay.”

Kara didn’t realize how kind Nia was. She realized how in her head she had been as she watched the young woman ramble on excitedly about the fashion article she was writing about the textile district. She had met a woman who had a studio there. She designed her own prints and was a staple of the city for the past three decades. Her hands waved and her eyes had a slight sparkle. Kara smiled, musing to herself about how bright the young woman was. She briefly pondered how she would have been at Nia’s age if her life was different. She did it occasionally, but it never amounted to anything. The journey can be painful but worth it. Once she decided to keep Audra, hell even when she found out, even if she broke down, she knew she was going to be a mother no matter what.

But still, Nia did look so cute with her endless energy and pure aura.

“How the hell did you land an interview with her?” Kara said incredulously, speaking after Nia finished her hurried, branching story. Sure, Cat had thrown Kara some sources she could use, a small network she could expand as she published more and more articles, but nothing like that. That kind of get would be impossible for her even now.

Nia shut down briefly, her eyes fleeting around briefly and she sipped her matcha latte. “I—we met at a group therapy session. I’ve known her since I was eighteen, actually. Miss Akimoto is a great woman. She’s a community organizer as well as an artist. And she and I have something in common that I find very comforting. Being young and having an elder is good.”

Kara’s mind flipped through what little knowledge she had. The name briefly rang a bell. Kara had been consuming many small publications since she started writing. She had wanted to take in different writing styles and looking for events she could go to for a small online article. She recognized the name from a publication Maggie showed her.

What was it? Oh! “She was in the Pacific Pride paper. I subscribe to that,” Kara said eagerly. “She’s amazing. Part of her proceeds goes to shelters for LGBT housing.” She saw how scrunched up Nia was, as if preparing for a train to hit her. “You’re trans like her, aren’t you, Nia?”

Nia flicked her thumb underneath the lip of her lid, it partially coming off with a dull click. Kara realized she was taking the leap. Or maybe got caught up in her own excitement.

Kara knew the next thing she said would affect Nia if inappropriate. “I’m glad you have someone to look at as a role model. Little girls need someone to look up to. I’m assuming you were little when you started out. It’s amazing you use your job to give back to your community, Nia. It beats writing about local politics and business like I do.” Kara quirked up the corner of her mouth in a wry grin.

Nia got flushed and she looked down, smiling. “Thank you, Kara. Means a lot to hear.”

“So, should we have red or white wine tonight?”

“How about champagne?” Nia sat back and sipped from her drink.

“Buyer’s choice. I’ll drink it.” Kara held her chai tea up briefly, indicating it was a deal and took a sip.

The conversation rolled on and Kara for a minute felt a fleeting bit of normalcy. She didn’t notice the plain-clothed cop sitting a few tables away, or the panic button on her purse, or the anxiety that clawed at her most waking hours. She wasn’t watching her watch, counting the hours she could get Audra and go home to be with her daughter.

Someone who had empathy and not pity—does a body good.

The California sun cut through Kara’s clothes, making her skin hot and sweat formed between her shoulder blades.

She squinted behind her protective glasses, looking at the furthest target. Mon-el walked over carrying two boxes of bullets. Seeing him in the corner of her eye snapped her from her focus. She looked over slowly. “Hm?”

“You ready to shoot?” He put the box down and then pulled two firearms from his belt.

“I don’t want to have a gun in the house with Audra,” Kara said.

“I just want you to know how to use one. And, I find this helps with frustration,” Mon-el said, smiling as he said the second part. He showed her how to load, take the safety on and off, unjam, and cock the gun before letting her square up. He helped her adjust her posture. Once he had stepped back and put on his earplugs, he gave her the thumbs up to start. She looked just over the sight, taking a deep breath in through her nose, and out through her mouth.

She saw the mounted target, an anthropomorphic outline with red circles at its vitals. She squeezed the tight trigger slowly, feeling the resistance and the cool metal. It was heavier than she thought it would be. She remembered seeing Jeremiah, holding it with both hands and wielding it at his hip before he opened the closet door.

The bang went, the barrel lighting up with a flash, a blink of an eye and there was a hole between the red target on the target’s head and their ear. She braced her arms after the kickback, holding the gun steady. She put it down on the table in front of her and looked at the hole.

“They might live, but they’d be down for the count,” Mon-el mused. “What’s on your mind, Kara?”

Everything. She furrowed her brow, taking a moment before she looked at him. “I’m thinking about Jeremiah,” she told him. Figured she might as well be precise, yet withholding. She wouldn’t talk about the recurring dream where she is the one outside the closet. That Audra is the scared little girl.

“Oh, are you afraid of the gun? Kara, I’m sorry I didn’t even think of that,” Mon-el said, stepping to her.

“No—it’s okay. I’m not like this because of that. I’ve just never held a gun. It’s weird. Heavy. This one is bigger than his was.” She picked up the black and metallic weapon.

“Kara, you’re safe. It won’t be like that again. I promise I won’t let it happen.” He put his hand on her shoulder, and she looked over at him. She didn’t know how to feel, seeing the concern and care in his face. She didn’t love Mon-el anymore, no, but she could appreciate having him back in her life. In Audra’s. It felt odd to know she was in his care and under his protection. Ironic this was what had to happen before he took care of her and Audra in such an up-close manner.

She took a deep breath and looked at the target. “I want to keep going.” She put her earplugs back in and picked up the handgun.

She brought it up and aimed. Her mind wasn’t full of remembering to fear the next few shots. She thought of Lex, and what he had done. To Clark, to her, to Lena…

She looked back at her last shot to see where it landed. She got one in the center of the chest target. She fired again, and again, and again. She blacked out a moment, a dull click, click, click taking her out of her head and putting her back into reality.

“You’re empty,” Mon-el said, taking off his earplugs. He let out a whistle as he looked at her target. “You’re a good shot, Kara. A natural.” He saw the line going up and down the body. Head, neck, chest, stomach, crotch.

She put the gun down and took off her eye protection, rubbing her eyes after putting them down. “That’s swell.”

“You sure you don’t want to get at least a fiver shooter? We’re right here. I can help you through it all.”

“No guns in my house. You need one for work, I don’t.”

“I have the rifle too. Don’t need that for work.”

“You what?”

“It is also locked up. Kara, it’s okay. I grew up hunting.” He patted her shoulder. “Want to go again?”

She clicked her tongue and looked at the target. She sighed. “Why not?” She put the safety on and worked to take out the magazine and put bullets in.

Kara began to wonder why she kept allowing Mon-el’s ideas. Maybe it was gratitude, maybe it was being nice, or maybe she was just too tired to give a shit anymore about where she was pulled. She sat on the bench as she wrapped her hands, looking around. Imra was there with Laramie and Audra. Audra was playing with Mon-el, the man letting the girl get a few punches in on his boxing mitts. She giggled excitedly as she got hit after hit in, Mon-el being easy on her and not making her tuck and dodge.

After the girl was satisfied and ran off to look at other equipment, Mon-el came over to Kara.

“I know you already do some stuff, so that'll be some fun sparring. Way to get the bad energy out and brush up beating up an opponent,” he said as he took the gloves off.

“Just like old times,” she said coolly, wrapping her hands in tape. When they were nineteen, they used to go to the gym together sometimes. Mon was much stronger than her back then. He was an athlete growing up, and Kara was a bookworm. She knew he probably saw that side of her still.

She flexed her fingers after she put her fingerless gloves on over her tape. “Yeah, I guess so.” He took the boxing mitts off and put on his wrappings and gloves. They got their headgear on and mouthguards in before they climbed into the small boxing ring nearby.

Kara squared up and looked at Mon-el. She could mainly only see his eyes. He had that usual amused smirk about him. He was also so sure of himself as if he never had to worry.

Kara watched as he took the first swing. She ducked his swing and stepped, pivoting on the ball of her left foot. She got back into her closed position, the two circling each other.

Kara got the next swing, but this time it landed. Mon was able to put his arms up in the nick of time, but he staggered at the force. He looked at her dumbfoundedly.

Kara smirked and moved again. She liked to feel this way. She felt the muscles in her legs twitch, ready to move and pounce. Her upper arm’s muscles popped as she was squared, and her neck was taught as the adrenaline started its way through her veins.

It was like the gun range, but different. It all faded away, but it was familiar. Organic. The range was Mon’s, but this was hers.

It was a careful dance between the two before Kara got into his space. She was locked on him, him struggling to protect his face and his forearms took the beating. He was grunting and trying to step back. He got into a corner and that’s when Kara let off, jumping back and planting herself back down onto the blood-stained floor of the ring.

The round ended and Kara took her headgear off. Mon-el ripped off his and then his gloves.

“You’re stronger than I thought,” he said, breathing heavily and almost stumbling.

Kara grinned and flexed her arms. “I maybe workout when I can,” she joked. She saw the bruises already starting to form on Mon-el’s arms and she saw the bit of hurt pride on his face. His lips were slightly pursed and he sat down next to Imra to pout a bit.

Audra ran to Kara and Kara scooped her up. “Mommy’s the best at fighting,” she said to her dad excitedly.

Kara couldn’t stop grinning.

Kara was packing up her bag on the bench as Imra corralled the kids as they prepared to leave. Mon-el approached her, his duffel bag resting against his hip. “You did really good up there. Kicked my ass,” he said. “I mean who taught you how to—”

“Alex decided that if I was going to be alone, I should be safe that way. And I agreed. I was alone, I had a baby, and I was living in slums half the time. Mon-el, you’re helping me and I’m grateful for that—but really, I’ve taken care of myself for a long time now. I’m taking police protection because a wanted domestic terrorist known for hiring hitmen threatened the safety of my daughter.” Kara finished packing up.

He sighed and looked around. “You always do this to me.” He looked down at her, his lips pressed into a thin line.

“Do what? Address the reality of our predicament? I forgive, I don’t forget, Mon-el.” She folded up her towel and put it in her bag.

“I don’t forgive myself, but I’m here now. That’s all that should matter.”

She looked at him. “Audra is all that matters, you’re right. But don’t lie and say you can’t forgive yourself. You went off and had another family. Obviously, you gave yourself enough grace to do that.” Kara put her bag over her shoulder. “Thank you for today and last week, Mon. But I think I really just want us to go back to how we were. We don’t have to become closer because you feel bad for me. I’m not going to go off the rails because I was forced to break up with my partner. I just need…to readjust. I’ve had to do it before. But you weren’t the people I needed to do that. I need a co-parent and a cop from you. Not a friend.”

Kara left with Audra, and Mon-el watched her go before doing what she said—taking his family and going.

Nia walked into Kara’s apartment holding the bottle of champagne and a stack of early-mid-2000s movies. DEBS, Watch but I’m a Cheerleader, and Legally Blonde were three titles out of them. It had become a semi-regular thing now.

“You won’t believe the eyeful I got when I was renting these,” Nia laughed, “They’re the worst and best movies of all time, I swear.”

“I haven’t seen this cheerleader one, but anything with Natasha Lyonne I will watch.”

Nia smiled at her as she opened the bottle. The small pop! was paired with the gentle clink of glasses as Kara got them out. The flutes she had were still dirty, so they drank champagne from kitschy mugs Kara had. They put on the cheerleader movie and had it in the background as they talked.

Nia was going on about this weird guy she met at the coffee shop. “He just kept staring at me really intensely. I didn’t know what to do. His hands were folded in a weird way. He was cute, but just…off-putting.”

“That sounds like a lot of guys around here. Welcome to the city.” Kara clinked glasses with her and sat back.

“Have you been seeing anyone since the split up?” Nia asked her, looking over at Kara.

Kara put her mug down. “No. I haven’t even thought of it. And no, I don’t need a wing woman. I just am going back to how I was before Lena. A mom. I’ll date when Audra’s out of the house.” Kara said the last part with a light-hearted smile, but Nia still looked at her with concern.

“She really did a number on you.” Nia scooted closer to her. “What exactly happened?”

Kara looked down at the floor, pulling her knees to her chest and hugging them. She leaned her back against the arm of the couch and she looked across at Nia. She thought of that night Lena first came over. They drank wine and talked for hours. Lena slept on her couch. It all felt too perfect.

This didn’t feel like that. Nothing ever would, would it? Sitting like it, a new image she felt like had been flipped. Not talking about reality made her feel even more alone.

What harm was there to speak?

“I can’t see her. Lex won’t let me. And since he’s free, he can do whatever he wants,” Kara said bluntly.

Nia looked at her. “Like her brother? The guy who tried to kill a journalist Lex?”

“Yes. The journalist was my cousin.” Kara took a long, long sip of her champagne and watched as Natasha Lyonne make out with Clea DuVall.

“Clark Kent is your cousin?” Nia asked her, amazed. “So, Lois Lane is too—why are you working for Cat when you could be working for the Daily Planet?”

“Clark and Lois have their own lives. Plus, Alex is in National City. Winn’s here. My family is here. Clark is just biologically related to me. He loves me, but not like Alex does.” Kara was able to muster a smile thinking of her. She needed to go see Alex. The due date was getting closer week by week.

“He’s keeping you from her? And you’re just gonna let him?” Nia asked, looking over at the other woman.

“I have no choice.”

“There’s always a choice. It just feels like you’re gonna lose it all to get it all. That’s how it feels when you’re about to change.” Nia scooted to her, putting her arms around her. Kara tensed at first, but eventually melted into Nia and touched her arm.

“Thank you, Nia. You’re—you’re braver than a lot of people I know.”

“Aren’t your sisters both cops?”

“Yeah. But I can match their type of brave. I don’t think I’ll ever match yours.”

Nia blushed a bit as she pulled from Kara. “Thanks.”

Kara looked at her. “Can we be brave together?” She put her hands on Nia's knees and leaned forward

Nia arched a brow, leaning back slightly. “What do you mean?”

“You knew what you wanted and damned the consequences. Right?”

“Yeah…but I also knew how to not get killed. Don’t tell everyone, blend in, gather information prior to everything.”

“We need to do that. I need to get what I want without being killed.” Kara got up and grabbed her laptop. “Picture this Nia—true crime journalist. Most wanted man in America. How about it?”

Nia grabbed the bottle of champagne, pouring another mug-full. “Kara, are you asking me for help with catching Lex Luthor? The man you’re supposed to be hiding from?”

“I know it’s crazy—”

Nia stood up and walked to Kara. “It’s the epitome of batshit,” she said quickly, a bit of her drink sloshing out the side of her “Best Mom” mug. She grinned. “So, I’m gonna help you do it.”

Notes for the Chapter:

I don't know if anyone notices (but I do because I'm neurotic about keeping characters "in character"), but initially I made Nia more "Cat Grant" like because I wrote her into this story before she was even on the show entirely, and that was the audition clipping that I saw. Nia in the series is quite the opposite. So, I think this is the most "her" I've tried to write her.

29. One Way, Or Another...

Summary for the Chapter:

Kara and Lena are on the case, they just don't know about the other.

Notes for the Chapter:

This is probably the longest chapter I've done for this fic yet. I could've made it 2-3 chapters, but Idk, it just seemed like it was better narrative-wise as a whole block.

Since Nia got to the city, she had been using her home as a storage unit and laundromat. She spent her time at work or in the 24-hour coffee shop a block from her apartment. She could sleep anywhere; it was her special talent. Her mother always teased her about it when she was little, but she was the exact same. She woke up now on Kara’s couch.

How did she wake up, one might ask? Oh, just Audra Danvers looking down at her. The little girl’s light brown, almost blonde hair was in pigtails, and she was in a Little Mermaid nightgown.

“Lena was the last person who slept on our couch. She ended up my mommy’s girlfriend. Are you mommy’s new girlfriend?” Audra asked her, frowning.

Nia gasped and she sat up, moving so she didn’t smack her head into the little girl’s.

“I—what? No! I’m just your mother’s friend. I’m Nia. You’re Audra, right?” Nia said, rubbing her eyes and blinking before smiling at Audra.

The little girl pouted as she nodded. She was hugging a penguin plush toy.

“Don’t worry, I’m not gonna date your mom. Honestly, I think she misses Lena,” Nia said lightly.

“I do too,” Audra said sadly, looking down. Nia nodded a bit, looking out the window of the living room. She saw the sunlight streaming in.

 Wait—what time is it?

Nia grabbed her phone and she fought cursing. “Where’s your mom, Audra?” Nia asked as she got up.

“She’s talking to Miss Cat,” Audra said. “She’s staying home with me today.”

I guess I’ll need an Uber. God, when did we fall asleep?

Nia saw the blankets she had draped on her and over on the coffee table was a legal pad, a laptop, an empty bottle of champagne, and apparently at one point they had ordered pizza, as there was an almost empty pizza box.

Kara came out into the living room, still on the phone. She saw Nia and waved briefly. “Yes, I’ll send you the next five hundred words by tonight. Thank you for calling Daily for me, by the way. You know Clark, he meddles. Yeah, thank you. I’ll tell her hi, yes. She’ll get in touch.” Kara hung up and looked at Nia.

“What did I miss?” Nia asked. She finally finished processing fear and her head started throbbing. “Oh my god, my head…” She closed her eyes and cradled her forehead.

“Oh, I woke up with Audra and did some work. Got the okay for us to work from home, sent Cat the first five hundred words of our story.”

“Wait—we started that thing? How did I even work?”

Kara waved her off and went to the kitchen. She came back with a coffee and a bottle of ibuprofen. “Here. You were somehow able to type, so I just dictated to you. I did everything else. I also got her to get Clark’s notes and tapes. They hold onto that stuff, and the authorities have copies. Since I can’t ask Clark or the authorities, I went somewhere else. Journalistic favors are better than any bureaucracy. They’re getting mailed to us.”

Nia took two and sipped the coffee. She sighed. “Okay, so we’re doing this, huh?”

“Unless you want out,” Kara said, sitting next to her. Audra came and sat in her lap. Kara just automatically went to rubbing the girl’s back. She didn’t seem to even realize it. Nia nodded for a moment, not talking and slightly spacing out.

“No, I’m in.” Nia took another, more determined sip. She looked at Kara. “What about keeping this under wraps? What if you’re bugged?”

“They search weekly. And it has to be complete. It’ll take time. We can catch him before the story, make it so no one but us and Cat knows. She’s good at keeping secrets, don’t worry.”

Nia sighed and got up. “Okay. Take me to my place so I can change clothes and get my laptop?”

“Yes, of course. Audra, we’re gonna be having a play date with Nia today. How does that sound?” Kara asked the young girl, looking down with a motherly smile and those signature sparkly blue eyes.

“Is she your girlfriend, mommy?” Audra asked again. Kara was taken aback briefly before frowning and then going to a goofy smile. It was so quick, Nia almost missed it. She sipped her coffee.

Kara laughed and she tickled Audra’s sides. “No, bean, Nia’s a friend from work. She and I are both writers and are working together. Mom’s not gonna get a boyfriend or girlfriend anytime soon.”

Audra nodded slowly and got up, running to play in her room. Kara looked at Nia, still smiling. “Sorry about that. She’s a very…forward child,” she said, her smiling growing bigger briefly when she finally found the word to describe her daughter.

“Did I hear you call her “bean?”” Nia arched a brow as she spoke in a dry, surprised tone.

“It’s a long story, but to make it short, it’s a nickname my friends came up with when I was pregnant with her.”

They got ready, Nia drinking her coffee and eating the doughnuts Kara had gotten while she was sleeping. Nia was impressed as she watched Kara go. She got the girl dressed and her hair done. Audra put on her shoes as she did it. She was well-dressed as well. She had a denim skort on with a little band shirt tucked in. Kara was dressed just as well. She had on ruby red dress pants, a white button-up, and a black cardigan. Her shoes were dark brown, and so was her belt. Her socks were obviously from Audra, as they were light blue with green pterodactyls. 

She had also made coffee and gotten doughnuts as well. But that had also meant she took Audra out while the girl was still in her pajamas. Sure, she wasn’t perfect., but she was damn good. But there was also this tired air around her, some of her smiles seeming forced or for Audra’s sake. But the only real ones also seemed to be only targeted at Audra.

They eventually left and the ride was mostly Kara playing Taylor Swift and answering Audra’s questions. Kara talked to her like she was talking to someone at work sometimes, but Kara was also nice at work. The girl did have the confidence to show for it.

Nia packed her stuff up and got changed. She shouted to her roommate that she would be at her co-worker’s place for another day at least while she washed her face.

“Are they cute?!”

Nia laughed. “She’s very hunky.”

“Ooh!”

She laughed to herself as dried off and finished up. She went back downstairs and got into Kara’s car. She saw a police car parked on the street across from them. She made eye contact with the one inside and she froze.

It’s that weird guy from the coffee shop. Nia looked in the car, and she saw that Kara was looked at her through the driver’s window, that being the part of the car that faced the door of the apartment building.

Nia got into the car. “So what’s the story with that guy? I saw him when we were at the coffee shop.”

Kara drove away, and when they were a few blocks away, Nia saw the same car take a left and pull up next to them. “It’s another long story.

“Can I get the long story?” Nia asked Kara. Kara stopped at the red light and looked at Nia. “Not here. Not now.” She turned up the music and drove off when the light changed.

They got to Kara’s place and Kara set up Audra with some coloring books and crayons at the kitchen table. Nia and she worked in the living room, a wall partially obscuring them, but an angle that gave Kara an easy view of her.

Kara got them water and another cup of coffee. “Which one first? The teen mom part or the cops follow me everywhere part?”

“Which one do you not want to do the least?”

Kara held her mug in both hands, taking a deep breath. “I met Mon-El freshman year of college. It’s tacky, really. He was a frat boy and I was the shy girl on a full-ride because she was good at math, but secretly loved writing. I never got guys to like me. Or girls for that matter, but I didn’t really know yet. That’s a story—oh, that’s a story I don’t want to tell.” Kara laughed.

“Ex-girlfriend drama, I get it. Don’t need to know,” Nia said, smiling. She sat back and listened.

“Well, we end up together. He makes me more focused on sports and socializing. I met Winn before him, though. He’s the best person I’ve met, you know? He was actually the one who gave her that nickname.”

“That sounds like something he’d do. It’s kinda ridiculous, but it’s sweet,” Nia said fondly.

“Yeah. But anyway, he and I date. We get into sex, like bunnies even though we fought quite a bit. I was in computer sciences, you know? I wanted to work in cybersecurity. But then I got pregnant, nineteen and sophomore year. He moves out of the country, off the continent. His mother tries to bribe me to get rid of Audra,” Kara said, her voice going lower the further she got into the explanation, her eyes sometimes flicking to her daughter.

“That’s so messed up.”

“Well, they’re rich. Like—almost Luthor rich, Nia. Audra has no rights to that stuff, but I don’t care. Mon-el pays now. He didn’t for a while. I had to move out of the quad because they wouldn’t let me have her there. It was a lot of struggling, staying with my sister while she was in med school. Her life kinda took a one-eighty like mine. She got her MD and then she joined the FBI.” Kara took a sip of coffee and Nia couldn’t. She just rubbed her finger around the rim of her mug as she listened to her. “I graduated and started working under Cat. You make more as an assistant with a degree, but funny enough you don’t need it to get the job. At least college was free for the most part.” She smiled. “So, here I am.”

Nia nodded. “Okay. What are we doing next?”

Kara looked at the notes they had handwritten. “I had an intern get the manuscripts from Lex’s trials. And Lillian’s. They think they’re smarter than everyone, they messed up somewhere and no one cared about it. I also have access still to records at L-Corp. I got it for my last article. I can keep looking. I just have to sneak in.”

Nia looked at her. “You think those cops are even going to let you near there? If Lex is watching her and wanting to keep you from her, is running into her company building, where she works every day, to get records. Records that might alert him we’re looking for him. You want that?”

“Yes. Thank you for understanding. You’ll watch Audra. I know seven is the best time. The shift change is happening so the security is shit. But the records room auto-lock at ten. So I have like an hour before anyone might find me and slip out after downloading what I want.”

“Watch her—Kara! I don’t hang around kids. This is a bad, bad idea,” Nia said frantically.

“You can put her in front of the television, don’t give her anything with sugar in it, and put her in bed at eight-thirty. Read until she falls asleep. She’s a well-behaved kid. She’s been used to a village for a while. That’s why I’m here. If you don’t want to, fine. I can just go off the very, very limited records I had from when I interviewed the girlfriend I lost to an evil man. I can cope.”

Nia groaned. “Damn, you play fiddle, Kara. Fine, I’ll do it. You just better be fast.” Nia ran her fingers through her hair. “Where did we get last night? It’s a blur, I’m impressed that I was any help.”

“You were amping me up quite a bit, that kinda helped.” Kara sat back down and grabbed her computer.

In Lena’s bedroom, the window is next to the bed. It was there so the sun won’t shine into her eyes, and so when Lena can sleep, she was able to sleep. The mid-morning sun barely bled into the large bedroom. Lena was curled up under her weighted blanket and was surrounded by papers and her laptop. She was pouring over financial records. She knew Lex was keeping his cash flow by hemorrhaging money from somewhere.

She still had to talk to Sam. Maybe that was why Lena woke up slowly, feeling the heavy dread in her chest keeping down her ability to get up. Her legs didn’t want to move when she thought about how she needed more help. She needed another tech mind. Sam knew finances. She could pour over records and track the money down to the cent. You’d think she was a forensic accountant. Lena’s first impression of Sam was the true representation of her work ethic.

She knew who she needed, but she paused. Was it betrayal? Was it dangerous?

She groaned and threw her blanket off of her, the dull thwap of the weighted blanket folding over itself due to its heft. She was stone sober at the moment. She had tried to be this whole time. She was ready for whatever could come. She knew Lex could bribe, kill, and hide to get to her. He always made sure she knew that.

She grabbed her phone off her nightstand where it was charging. She dialed Sam and got on some casual clothes before Sam picked up.

“Hello? Lena?” Sam said, her pitching rising slightly in concern.

“Hey—I need to talk to you. You busy?” Lena asked as she buckled her belt. She could hear the rushing of traffic and some bumping. “You in the car?”

“Yeah, I just dropped Ruby off at camp. Her last adventure before going into freshman year. Gah—I can’t believe she’s getting so old,” Sam said. “But I have three weeks of just one thing: M-E. I’m gonna do a half-day and then go home. How about you, darling? How are you holding up?”

There it is. “I’m working on something having to do with my situation.” Lena paused, wetting her lips and swallowing. “You were right, okay?”

“I know I am.” Sam took a dramatic pause. “I have a ticket. I’m able to stay as long as you need, as long as it’s less than three weeks,” she said in a casual tone, but Lena could hear the smile she had to have had plastered on her face.

“What? You cannot—”

“You got broken up with and going rogue. I have to be with you. Jess can’t be the only person keeping you from going insane,” Sam said, her voice commanding.

“I’ll email you what I need you to bring then. When and where am I picking you up?”

“I’ll text you when I get to the airport. I’m on my way to the office now. I’ll talk to you later.”

“Fine. Be safe.”

“You too. Don’t do anything stupid before I get there.”  

“I’m trying really hard.” The two laughed and ended the call. Lena looked at the papers in front of her. Would this be what she would classify as crazy?

She took a shower and got dressed. She got ready for work, meeting her guards and the car downstairs when she was ready to leave. She ordered her and Jess’ drinks from their usual place. She swiped away a text from Cat Grant inviting her for brunch. Sure, she’d get a call later because Cat Grant will not be ignored. Lena was in the mood to ignore things at the moment, Cat Grant was the future Lena’s problem.

She didn’t talk with Ernest, but she could see him watching her with the rearview. He was always that type—the hovering, paternal type. He cleared his throat when he pulled up to the curb. She got out and paid the meter before going into the shop. She went to the pick-up counter when she heard it.

“Chai, hot chocolate, and matcha for Kara!”
