Lena put her sunglasses on and grabbed her order, and zipped to the door. She paused a moment, looking around. She saw her, and she looked the same. She was dressed more masculine—a blue blazer and black shirt, and blue dress pants. Her hair was down and she wasn’t wearing glasses. She looked handsome. Radiant. Gorgeous in a way that felt like someone was daydreaming. Lena couldn’t romanticize Kara, because she was truly that amazing.

She saw Kara grab the drinks and looked towards the bar built by the window. She saw a young brunette woman sitting with her. She was wearing a sundress and was clicking away at the keyboard of her laptop. Audra was with them, sitting in a chair and kicking her legs as she colored. The woman looked up and smiled when Kara approached her.

Lena took a deep breath and stepped out, going to her car. She got in her car and exhaled. “I’m ready. Thank you.”

She sat there in silence, putting up the partition. She mumbled the periodic table to herself while she watched the city zip by her, distracting her mind from her anxiety. By the time she got to work, she was perfectly calm. She got upstairs, greeted by several members of security in the meantime. She got up to her floor and took a few turns to the grand hall that led to her white doors. She saw that Jess was away from her desk. Must be printing something or in the bathroom. She put the tea down for Jess and went into her office.

“Mr. Schott—”

“No, this-this is a bad idea. I should go. I should go.” Winn was speaking frantically as Jess tried to keep him from leaving.

“Winn,” Lena said, raising her voice slightly. Winn looked at her and calmed down. “Jess, you can go. I’m sorry for the trouble. Your tea is on your desk.”

Jess nodded and left. Winn looked at Lena. “Why did you call me?” He asked in a high, slightly frantic voice.

Lena walked to her desk, sitting on the edge of it. She crossed her legs, her Louboutin heels flash. She took a long sip of her triple expresso americano. “I’m in over my head,” she said with a wry grin.

“Oh no, Lena,” he sighed, sitting in the chair in front of her desk. “This is bad. This is very bad. We shouldn’t be doing this. What am I gonna tell Kara—”

“Relax! I’m asking for a favor, not an affair.” She took another sip as she got up and sat behind her desk. “Come over here.”

Winn paused, gripping the armrests of the chair and biting the inside of his cheek. He knew that what Lena was doing might not be good, but with good intention. He got up and came over, bringing the chair with him and flumping down into it after putting it down.

“Lex needs endless funds, and he knows how to get that. It’s like you said to me once—the more complex a system gets, the more vulnerable it is. I figure that since the system has so many levels and fail-safes, different accounts, and passages that it’s too dense to find him. He’s in the system. But I own the system,” she said, gesturing. She sighed and looked at him. “I need you to make it simple. A crumb.”

Winn rubbed the back of his neck and looked at the screen. “Can I sit in your spot?” She got up and he slid over into her desk chair, raising it slightly. “Get me a coffee and, I don’t know—sweet talk Cat. She seems to like you more than she likes me. Class solidarity?” Winn looked over his shoulder and grinned.

She glared briefly, looking up from her phone. She then smiled a bit. “You think she’s as rich as me, Winn? That’s cute. Let me put it this way—she has so much less than me that if he was stealing from her, she’d have noticed. I am working on a hunch because the millions he needs is a crumb,” she said in a slightly mocking tone.

“Oh, I hit a sensitive spot?” He teased, not looking at her. He got a small slap to the back of the head. “So how am I getting paid for this?”

“Oh, I’m not paying you.” She sipped her coffee, holding her hand up to stop him from speaking. “A lawyer with an anonymous client that’s using a trust in Zurich, Switzerland is paying you. So—proceed. Also, your phone won’t work in here so make calls on the balcony. Extraordinary measures.”

“God, Lena.” He started digging through it all, seeing the keys she left him with. There was a whole manual that she had locked up in her office. From where he didn’t know.

Lena stepped out to call Cat. She got her expected earful and she had to concede to brunch where they went out. Luckily Cat didn’t pry this time. Though she knew that once they were in-person, she would be interrogated. Cat Grant was effective no matter the medium, but she was best up close and personal.

When she came back in, she saw Winn still looking at the manual. He held it open with one hand as he scrolled around the computer’s expansive network with the other. He seemed in awe, like a little kid that’s just walked into a toy shop. “I’ve never seen this. No government agency I’ve hacked has had this much security,” he said, off-handedly adding the second part, too distracted to care about incriminating himself.

“Pardon me?” Lena smirked and arched a brow.

“Nothing. Just messing with you,” Winn said quickly. He smiled at her.

“Whatever. I’m in no place to be a snitch.” Lena looked out her window. There were a few beats of silence from both, the only sound being the ice in her drink whenever she took a sip, the shuffling of pages, and the click of keys. Until Lena finally broke and she spoke, “she was at the shop. Who is she working on a story with? I saw her with a woman. A pretty one.”

Winn looked up and looked over at her. “Lena, if this is all just to learn how she’s doing, I’ll leave.”

“No! no.” Lena looked at him and frowned. “Please just tell me she hasn’t moved on.”

Winn set his jaw and turned back to the computer. “She doesn’t sleep, she gets nightmares. She’s in love with you. She’s single still.”

Lena nodded and sipped her coffee, looking ahead still. “Thank you. I…I’m coping. I’m doing this.”

“Alone. She’s doing something I know she shouldn’t.” Winn flipped a page in the book she gave him. “But she’s not doing it alone. I know Nia is going to do what she can and that there are at least two plain-clothed cops in public with her at all times. It’s overkill—”

“No. It isn’t. You tease about how easy it is to get into a government website, try getting into a government building and taking it over. Lex can do anything he wants, and he lacks the inhibition of someone with humanity. I know my brother,” Lena said coldly, her voice going hushed and she finally looked away from the skyline of National City.

“I think she’s coping too,” he said, wanting to change the topic.  Winn got back to working on the computer. “She still smiles. You know the one. And Audra is okay. She likes all the attention, I think. She doesn’t fully understand why mom has so many new friends and that she’s allowed in the office more.”

“Cat doesn’t ask questions?”

Winn smiled. “It’s Audra. You think she’s gonna get a no from anyone?”

Lena laughed softly. “You’re right. I’m not one to talk. Kara had to stop me from getting Audra whatever she wanted.”

“I know. I noticed the new toy every time I saw her.” Winn stopped himself and went silent, his smile disappearing.

“I don’t just miss them. I miss you guys. How’s Alex and the baby?”

Winn took a pause and he finally gave in. “Alex is due soon. They found out they’re having a girl. No big gender reveal. Maggie’s excited as hell. It’s surprising to me. James and I moved in together. Mon-el is Mon-el about all of this.”

“His mom isn’t being an ass, is she?”

“Rhea? No. She’s a non-character right now, at least.” Winn grimaced at the thought of her, his impressions of her poor on top of being few and far between.

Lena nodded again, struggling to form words. She acted like the view and her americano were the most important things to her right now. She acted like she wasn’t hanging onto every word, every scrap of information he could give her.

She sat with him, working on going through paper records with a fine-tooth comb. It was when the sun began to go down and when he began making more noises and stretching more often that Lena told Winn to go home. He just nodded and packed up silently. Before he left a random lawyer from Lena’s staff came in and gave Winn his check. Then security took him and got him into a cab.

Lena was bent over her desk and drinking a cup of tea when Jess came in to tell her Sam had just landed, as Lena had given Jess her phone hours ago. She got her phone back and had her car come around. She got in and sat back, scrolling through the messages she had missed as Ernest drove. They got to Sam’s gate and eventually, Ernest got out to help Sam load her bags into the trunk. Sam got in next to Lena and hugged her.

“Oh, how are you?” She squeezed her. “Are you in trouble?”

“It’s the usual characters,” Lena said as they parted.

“Where to now, Lena?” Ernest asked after he got back into his seat and buckled in.

“We’re going home. Sam and I have some catching up to do.”

“Of course,” he said, smiling at them through the rearview before putting up the partition. He pulled onto the road and they were off to Lena’s apartment.

Sam looked over at Lena, “so are you going to tell me now or do you want to see photos of your niece?”

Lena smiled. “I’ll take the latter.” She leaned in as Sam took her phone out and pulled up her photo album. She showed Lena photos from the day Ruby was starting camp. One at the sign at the entrance of the camp, Ruby flashing a toothy smile. Then there was in front of her cabin and on her bunk.

“Once she was settled in, I had to go. And now, three weeks of nothing,” Sam said as she put her phone away. She sat back. “A blessing or a curse, I can’t tell you.”

Ernest pulled up to Lena’s building and got out to help Sam with her bags. Once they were upstairs and the bags were set by the door, the older man stepped back and smiled at the two. “Will you be going anywhere else today, Miss Luthor?”

“No. Thank you, Ernest. I appreciate your patience and help today,” Lena said, smiling. She put her hands on his shoulders. “I don’t pay you enough. Next month, we’ll talk. Tell me what sounds good to you.”

His eyes widened a bit and then he smiled. “I’ll think on it, boss. Look forward to discussing it. My wife really has been eyeing a place on the beach to live now that the kids are all out of the house.”

Lena nodded, smiling back. “You two would certainly deserve it. You have a goodnight.”  Ernest disappeared into the darkness of the hallway as Sam and Lena entered her office.

Sam’s eyes widened at the sight of all the cases of papers and she also noticed a stack of hard drives on Lena’s desk.

“Who stole from us?” Sam looked at Lena.

“I don’t know. But I think Lex is, somehow. He has to be getting money somehow. If he can afford to travel wherever and hire goons to come after me. He’s been on the run for several years. Has to be somewhere,” Lena said, sauntering to her desk as she spoke.

Sam took her messenger bag off her shoulder and sat at her couch across the room. “Lena, why not turn this to the FBI or something? They have forensic accountants. Hell, hire a private firm.”

“No, because once it becomes bigger than me, he finds it. If he knows I’m looking, it’s bets off. He’s gonna freak if he does, and I can handle the annual death threats or near misses—tells me my brother’s alive. But he’s not predictable anymore. Does he try to hurt me or hurt…”

“The woman you’re in love with and her kid, yeah.” Sam sighed and rubbed her temple, her brow furrowing as well as she looked at the boxes. “What are we looking for?”

“I already have narrowed these boxes down. Of course, the time range is from when he went on the lamb to our most recent reports, so for last month. From there, I eliminated any expenses that were automatic, signed off by you or me, and any that were sent to domestic banks or financial entities. So really what we’re looking at are the expenses that could be suspicious, but don’t raise alarm. I’m not looking to find someone skimming off the company. I want crumbs. Lex is a lot of things, but he’s not perfect. Sometimes he leaves the crumb intentionally, sometimes he messes up. But I know one thing—he messes up in the same ways. He’s insane, he has these compulsions and plans. Once it’s in his head, he can’t let it go. That’s why he’s doing this, Sam. He knows that he could get caught doing this. But he can’t help it. He needs to be in control. He needs to make sure those who slighted him are miserable. Even if not leaving well enough alone could get him caught. If Lex really didn’t want to be found, we wouldn’t find him.”  

Sam looked at her as she talked, unexpressive. After Lena finished, she took a breath, sighing. Sam looked around at the boxes sprawled out. “I know your brother is fucking crazy. But Lena, I—so are you.”

Lena nodded and looked back at her. She played with her fingers, counting the knuckles and rubbing them. “I know. But at least my crazy is constructive.” She told Sam what to look for. Certain numbers that Lex liked to use, dates around times he did things, and countries she knew for a fact he had used to launder mail and money.

Sam worked on the papers. Lena worked through the hard drives. Sam stopped suddenly and she looked in a box. “Wait…that’s weird.”

“What’s up?” Lena looked up from her laptop. She straightened her back, rubbing one of her shoulders.

Sam was still rifling through the box. “I’m missing a month.”

“Which one?” Lena got up and walked over.

“Last month,” Sam said.

Lena’s face paled. “No…how?”

Don’t get her wrong, Kara was a self-aware person. She knew when something was wrong, and she had the confidence to know when to turn back or go on.

But God, if this moment was so stupid of her because she wasn’t turning back. Nia and she had gotten into the business building. L-Corp was partially open to the public. Entering the door didn’t mean you immediately were interrogated. However, if you wanted to do more than just look at the atrium along with the statue of the company’s logo, you had to go to the security desk.

Kara strutted confidently to the desk and smiled, a pink box under her arm. Confidence, Kara. Sell it like you believe it.

“Rob, a pleasure,” Kara said, her voice rising in the middle so she seemed more upbeat.

The middle-aged man raised his head up from the computer that had screens up. Kara had seen the screen many times between how many times she came here for lunch and interviews. She had gotten to know the staff, there was no hiding and using an alias. She just had to act like she belonged there still.

“Hey, Kara. I haven’t seen you around in a while. My, no wonder Miss Luthor’s been all gloomy. Where the hell were you?” He asked, smiling. Rob was a special guy. A former US Marine, he had the look of the usual jarhead. Meaty neck, clean-shaven and short hair, and a jaw so square he could use it as a straight edge. Then he opened his mouth and you got the thick Long Island accent and his hazel eyes would light up like a little kid’s if he was happy to see you. He also had a weakness for Eclairs.

“Oh, just stayed with family for a while. Looks different around here.” She looked around, seeing the private security Lena had hired. She saw a gun on a few of them. They were all serious-looking, wore the same uniform of black polos—Kara could see they all had vests under them—and gray slacks. They flanked most doorways, and she saw that when the elevator opened, there was one that stayed on even as people shuffled out, and did that several times.

It was locked down. Did Lena really fear Lex that much? Did he really warrant that much danger?

Kara had only had some cops follow, and they got more casual as time went on without any major threats. After all, she had been following Lex’s rules. He was just holding up his end of the bargain, she supposed. She had been demoted to one or two plain-clothed officers with her in public, and security at work still checked in on her a few times a day. Ducking her escort was easy. Nia and she went to a coffee shop a block from the building with Audra, and Nia flirted with the oblivious cop that sat with them while Kara slipped out the door by the bathrooms.

Kara shook off her sudden spike in anxiety and she kept selling her spiel. She eventually finished the small talk and he let her go without asking her to state her business or having to call up to Jess.

Kara let out a sigh of relief as she walked, taking a left where she would have taken a right if she was trying to see Lena. She got to the records room and quickly put in the punch code. Her hands shook as she did, her hearing a squeaky wheel and footsteps approach. She shoved the door open and shut it behind her. She took in deep breaths of the dry air inside the temperature-controlled room. She turned on the flashlight she had. It was all plastic so it didn’t show up in the detectors. She knew that she was looking at a white whale’s worth of paper, but she had a hunch. She knew that any significant amount she took would cause red flags. So, she and Nia spent a few days deliberating on what criteria of information they needed. They settled on was there any suspicious or unreported expenses during the month of a known incident caused by Lex.

The month of July of that year was what Kara was gonna take. She grabbed the brown cardboard box labeled "May-July 20XX." She rifled through, grabbing the month of July. She put a thick rubber band on it before shoving their paperwork into the tote bag she brought (and the reason the pastries were pivotal to her plan).  She checked her watch and took a few deep breaths before she stood up. She listened through the door to make sure no one was outside before she dashed out and went into the staircase. She ran up to a random floor and into the bathroom adjacent to the door she exited through. She locked the door behind her and tied her hair up.

She put her bag up on the counter, unzipping it to retrieve her partial change of clothes. Was her plan convoluted? Yes. Did she think it would keep Lena or Lex from seeing her? 50/50. Nia and she were smart about this, but she knew she was no Lex. And she certainly was not Lena. 

Ten minutes passed, each one Kara’s heart pulsing in her throat. Still, she managed her change. Contacts first. Then she took off her blazer for the blouse underneath. She undid a button and rolled up the sleeves. She pulled her slacks off, underneath she had put on tights. She put on a skirt and then heels. Lipstick last and she paused one moment. Lena saw her as two different people, and Kara always teased her about it. But at the moment, Kara understood. She didn’t recognize the woman in the mirror as her. Just a little, but enough. To not be known as herself to strangers.

 She cleaned up and unlocked the door. She slipped out and got out. She looked around the hall she had randomly chosen. She saw about thirty feet to her right was a turn. Ahead of her was another expansive hall that led to a door. To her left was the staircase she had exited on. And just across, there was the elevator entrance.

Before Kara could make a choice, she heard footsteps. The click, click, click that echoed, signifying a woman in high heels and she was walking quickly. She heard the voice, and it was just as powerful and distinct as those heels.

“…I'm on my way to you now. You just sit tight. Grab a coffee. It's going to be a long night.”

Kara’s blood ran cold, but yet the pulse in her throat was a hot coal right against her jugular. She clicked on the button to the elevator a few times. It opened to a crowded one and she elbowed into the back corner after telling the man in front of the buttons what floor she was going to.

She was situated next to a woman in more casual attire than everyone else in the elevator, but she had an employee ID on her shirt. The woman looked at Kara when she got close. “You seem like you’re in a rush.”

“I got in here to escape my ex,” Kara said jokingly, smiling nervously and laughing briefly.

The doors were going to draw close as Kara began to let out her breath.

“Hold the door!” That same distinct voice called, eventually a pale arm thrusting between the doors and a manicured hand holding the door.

Lena Luthor stepped in and stood in the front and center, people parting for her and shuffling back as if Lena needed a perimeter. Lena told the man what floor she needed and then looked at her phone. The woman looked at Kara with a raised eyebrow. She motioned her head at Lena, and Kara nodded.

Kara tried to be as small as she could, as quiet as possible. No deep breaths, no clearing her throat, and not tapping her foot. The woman kept staring at Kara. Kara looked and saw that ID again. “Elise Delano. Chief of Cybersecurity”

Elise moved to Lena, speaking up, “Miss Luthor?”

Lena looked up from her phone and at Elise, the two the same height. Elise smiled. “Hello…Elise.” It came to Lena after a second, her eyes trained on Elise’s face the whole time and not straying to her badge. “What can I do for you?” It was a talent Kara knew Lena had. She knew all her employees from her VP down to the janitor that cleaned the laboratory in the basement. 

“Oh, well I know we’re due for a meeting about your concerns about the recent update to our security, so I wanted to ask what you were expecting in the reports. You know, make sure we all got what we need and there’s no need for that whole run around after a meeting to tie up the loose ends and answer questions. I like my ducks in a row, you know?” She rambled on, Lena nodding along and eventually had to get off. Elise looked at Kara and gave her a thumbs-up before also getting off, but taking the opposite direction to Lena.

Kara finally fully let out a breath and leaned against the wall. A few more stops and she got out, leaving L-Corp and walking around the corner to get into her car. She drove to her apartment, checking her watch briefly at a light. Audra would be asleep if things had gone to plan. 

She got back into her apartment to see Audra asleep on the couch and Nia drinking gin in her kitchen. 

"She didn't recognize me as an authority," Nia said with a sigh when Kara laid eyes on her, the mother wearing a look of pure bewilderment. 

"I gave you an impossible task, it's fine," Kara said, putting her bag down and kicking off her heels. "I got the month." 

"Sweet." Kara held out her hand. "What? Oh, sorry for drinking your booze." She handed Kara the glass. 

Kara finished it off with a long sip and put the glass down. "Grab another. I'll put her in her bed, we'll tear through these documents, and finish that bottle. Please." Kara let her hair down. 

"I can live with that." Nia let out another sigh. This one loud, dramatic, and tired. "This is why I don't want kids." She laid her head on the table. "Sorry." 

"None taken. I was a teen mom. It's not technically supposed to happen, but it did. Just make sure your condoms aren't expired." 

Nia snorted. "That's not my concern, but thank you." 

Kara stopped and laughed softly. "Right. I forgot." She took Audra to bed, able to handle her without waking her up. She got the girl down and went back to the kitchen. 

Nia was already taking the rubber band off the stack of papers. There were two glasses of gin now. 

"Okay, let's get started." Kara sat back down next to her. 

Notes for the Chapter:

Should I change the summary now? I know that this has kinda taken a turn towards a thriller away from a fluff fic, but IDK, unless I've created a trigger scenario, I might leave it. If anyone feels like they would've liked a warning, let me know and I'll adjust the tags and summary.
